# AgentifyMe AI Agent Implementation Plan — DKMS Durable Knowledge Management Service

**Date:** 2026-10-03  
**Status:** V2 — user decisions applied; ready for Hermes execution  
**Primary executor:** Hermes / STEA-004 running GLM 5.3 Flash  
**Implementation worker:** Claude Code skill using Claude Opus 5.5, medium reasoning  
**Primary objective:** Create and integrate an isolated **Durable Knowledge Management Service (DKMS)** so valuable agent knowledge is no longer treated as an ACMS-owned durable-memory responsibility.

---

# 0. Mission

Build the first production-usable DKMS slice.

The service must preserve **useful, durable, attributable knowledge** produced by agents and the systems around them without trying to copy every byte of every system into one database.

The most valuable first knowledge classes are:

- approved AI-agent instruction plans;
- final handoffs and durable summaries of handoffs;
- architecture decisions and ADR references;
- Jira scope snapshots and completion summaries;
- work/project trajectory summaries;
- failure signatures, recovery attempts, root-cause findings, side effects, and lessons learned;
- successful remediation/playbook knowledge;
- model/agent evaluation findings that explain why work succeeded or failed;
- selected agent knowledge that should survive runtime/session replacement;
- curated records that may later become training/evaluation data.

The DKMS is **not** a replacement for Jira, GitHub, ACMS, Server Manager, LLM Manager, PDU Manager, Hermes state backup, or service-specific telemetry.

The DKMS is the durable knowledge layer that those systems can write to and retrieve from.

---

# 0.1 User decisions applied in V2

[x] Canonical repository name: `startupteams/dkms-project-framework`.

[x] Full approved plans/handoffs/raw source default retention: **90 days**; durable summaries/trajectory knowledge retained indefinitely.

[x] ACMS keeps the bulk of raw operational conversation/session information; DKMS stores selected high-value knowledge and summaries rather than all raw data.

[x] Registered STEA agents may write useful knowledge/lessons directly to DKMS outside an ACMS Work Item through scoped MCP.

[x] **Validated internal failure/recovery lessons may be automatically training-approved.** The system must distinguish automatic approval from human approval and record whether human review occurred.

[x] DKMS gets a **new dedicated VM** and a **dedicated Kubernetes service**. It does not run inside the ACMS container.

[x] ACMS must support agents/operators logging **Lessons Learned** to DKMS.

[x] DKMS is **not primarily a Work-statistics database**. ACMS owns detailed Work statistics/status. DKMS stores lessons, architecture/implementation trajectory, selected handoff knowledge, and concise dated Jira/work summaries useful for understanding what changed and what work may logically come next.

[x] The next ~15 instruction files remain predominantly internal reliability/security/evaluation/infrastructure work before serious external Speed-to-Lead expansion.

---

# 3. Superseding architecture decision: ACMS is not the durable knowledge database

Treat this as the new working split:

```text
Human / approved plans
        |
        v
Jira ----------------------------+
| authoritative scoped work      |
| when a Jira issue exists       |
+--------------------------------+
        |
        v
ACMS
| Work / assignment / state / controls / operational events
| recent chat and streaming work notes
| references to durable knowledge
        |
        +-----------------------> DKMS
                                  | approved plans
                                  | handoff source + summary
                                  | ADR / architecture trajectory
                                  | Jira scope/completion snapshots
                                  | failure/recovery learning
                                  | evaluation findings
                                  | agent lessons
                                  | training candidates
                                  | context-pack source records
                                  |
                                  +--> MCP namespace: dkms.*
                                  +--> Markdown export
                                  +--> future training/eval export

Server Manager / LLM Manager / PDU / Hermes / GitHub
        |
        +-----------------------> selected high-value facts and references
```

## 3.1 ACMS continues to own

- Work Item identity and status;
- assignments;
- durable Agent UID relationships;
- operational control state;
- recent execution/session events;
- recent chat/streaming notes needed for operating current work;
- attention and policy workflow;
- work/project/product relationships;
- links to DKMS knowledge records.

## 3.2 DKMS owns

- durable knowledge objects and their versions;
- provenance and source references;
- durable summaries;
- failure-learning records;
- architecture/decision trajectory;
- selected requirement/scope snapshots;
- selected agent lessons;
- training/evaluation eligibility metadata;
- retrieval/context-pack inputs;
- human-readable Markdown exports.

## 3.3 Other systems remain authoritative for their domains

- **Jira:** human-scoped work and externally visible result/status when a Jira issue exists.
- **GitHub:** source code, PRs, commits, repository-native docs.
- **Server Manager:** runtime placement/lifecycle and runtime-recovery execution.
- **LLM Manager:** model availability/routing/usage/benchmark facts.
- **PDU Manager:** physical-power authority.
- **Hermes profile/state repository:** complete harness/profile state until a future state architecture replaces it.
- **DKMS:** curated durable knowledge derived from and linked to these systems.

Do not copy entire source systems into DKMS merely because an API exists.

---

# 4. Executor contract

## 4.1 Hermes / GLM 5.3 Flash

Hermes is the orchestrator and tracker.

Hermes must:

- read this plan and the latest handoff before changing code;
- maintain the objective and acceptance matrix;
- use the Claude Code skill for bounded implementation work;
- keep one implementation writer per branch;
- checkpoint between major phases;
- persist progress before `/new`, model limits, or context exhaustion;
- track Jira / Work / repository / PR / deployment / DKMS record IDs;
- provide a final Markdown handoff.

Hermes should not become the primary coder merely because a change is small if the plan expects Claude Code implementation.

## 4.2 Claude Code / Opus 5.5 medium

The requested implementation model is **Claude Opus 5.5, medium reasoning**.

At the start of the run:

1. record the requested model;
2. record the effective model actually returned by Claude Code;
3. if Opus 5.5 is unavailable, do not silently substitute another paid API/model;
4. checkpoint the exact limitation and ask for a model-policy decision unless the existing approved configuration explicitly defines a fallback.

Each Claude packet must contain:

- Work UID and Jira key;
- repository and branch;
- objective;
- allowed scope;
- current phase;
- acceptance criteria;
- known constraints;
- rollback requirement;
- source pointers;
- **no secret values**.

---

# 5. Security rule: secrets are not knowledge

DKMS must never become a secret dumping ground.

Do not ingest:

- passwords;
- API keys;
- bearer tokens;
- cookies;
- private keys;
- raw credential files;
- environment files containing secrets;
- screenshots or logs that expose secrets.

Knowledge may contain a **secret reference**, credential name, secret-store path, or statement that a credential exists, but not the secret value.

Before a record becomes training-eligible, it must pass secret/sensitive-data checks.

If ingestion detects likely secret material:

```text
reject or quarantine
→ log metadata only
→ mark training_eligibility = deny
→ provide a sanitized-ingestion path
```

Do not echo any secret values from historical source files into Jira, DKMS, logs, prompts, handoffs, screenshots, or Git.

---

# 6. DKMS MVP architecture

## 6.1 Repository

**User decision:** the canonical repository name is approved as:

`startupteams/dkms-project-framework`

At execution start, verify that this exact repository does not already exist. If absent, create **this exact repository** under the Startup Teams organization; do not create a differently named substitute.

Initialize it using the Startup Teams project-framework conventions with at minimum:

- `README.md`;
- architecture/service-boundary documentation;
- requirements;
- future work / technical debt;
- ADR directory;
- deployment/runbook documentation;
- tests and CI structure appropriate to the chosen implementation.

Repository creation is part of P0/P1 acceptance, not a future naming decision.

Use the existing Startup Teams project-framework documentation conventions where practical.

## 6.2 Service boundary

DKMS must be independently deployable from ACMS.

Default MVP:

```text
DKMS service
  Python / FastAPI
  Async SQLAlchemy
  PostgreSQL
  migration-managed schema
  internal REST API
  Markdown import/export
  exact/metadata/full-text retrieval
  audit events
```

Avoid a vector database in the MVP. The current architecture direction explicitly defers vector/semantic indexing until metadata/exact/full-text retrieval proves inadequate.

## 6.3 Hosting and Kubernetes deployment

**User decision:** DKMS gets a **new dedicated virtual machine** and a **dedicated Kubernetes service**. It does **not** belong inside the ACMS container.

MVP deployment direction:

```text
new dedicated DKMS VM
  -> lightweight Kubernetes distribution (prefer current standard; K3s is acceptable if no standard exists)
      -> dedicated `dkms` namespace
          -> DKMS application Deployment/Service
          -> PostgreSQL StatefulSet or equivalently durable Kubernetes-managed database deployment
          -> PersistentVolume-backed data
          -> migration job
          -> backup/export jobs
```

Requirements:

- provision the VM through Server Manager/current placement policy;
- do not hardcode a Proxmox node unless live placement policy requires it;
- do not place by bypassing protected-host rules;
- do not change hostnames;
- use a dedicated service account/namespace and least-privilege Kubernetes RBAC;
- internal LAN only; no public ingress;
- proposed internal service name: `dkms.miam.home.arpa`;
- register the VM/service in registry/monitoring when available;
- cover the VM with PBS according to current backup policy;
- add logical PostgreSQL backup/export in addition to VM backup;
- keep Kubernetes manifests/Helm/Kustomize configuration in Git so the service can be recreated;
- design the manifests so they can move to a multi-node cluster later without making multi-node Kubernetes a prerequisite now.

This is an explicit **DKMS-specific exception** to the broader architecture's previous “defer Kubernetes until scale pain” posture. It does not automatically migrate ACMS, Server Manager, LLM Manager, or the agent fleet to Kubernetes.

## 6.4 MCP access

Add a DKMS adapter/domain to the existing internal MCP gateway rather than creating an unrelated control plane.

Namespace:

`dkms.*`

Initial tools should be narrow and schema-validated.

Suggested read tools:

```text
dkms.get
dkms.search
dkms.list_recent
dkms.list_related
dkms.context_pack
```

Suggested safe-write tools:

```text
dkms.ingest_plan
dkms.ingest_handoff
dkms.record_timeline_entry
dkms.record_failure
dkms.record_recovery_attempt
dkms.record_lesson
dkms.record_decision
dkms.mark_training_eligibility
```

`dkms.record_lesson` must support both ACMS-dispatched agents and registered STEA agents operating outside an ACMS Work Item, provided identity/provenance/scope are recorded.

Administrative/destructive tools should not be introduced in the first slice unless required for retention testing.

Deletion should normally be retention-policy driven or explicit administrative action with audit.

---

# 7. Core data model

Use a small, explicit schema rather than a generic untyped blob store.

## 7.1 `knowledge_record`

Minimum fields:

```text
knowledge_uid
record_type
title
body_markdown / normalized text
content_sha256
source_kind
source_uri
source_uid
source_version
source_created_at
project_uid
product_uid
work_uid
jira_key
agent_uid
session_uid
repository
commit_sha
authority_class
confidence
sensitivity_class
training_eligibility
training_approval_mode
training_review_state
training_eligibility_reason
retention_class
created_at
updated_at
supersedes_uid
status
tags
```

Recommended `record_type` values for MVP:

```text
execution_plan
handoff_source
handoff_summary
architecture_decision
requirement_snapshot
implementation_timeline_entry
jira_scope_summary
jira_completion_summary
failure_event
recovery_attempt
lesson_learned
runbook
evaluation_finding
agent_knowledge
training_candidate
```

`implementation_timeline_entry` is intentionally a **short knowledge/timeline record**, not a copy of ACMS Work statistics. ACMS remains authoritative for detailed Work status, timing, assignments, and operational metrics.

Do not make every type mandatory in the first migration. Implement the types required by live acceptance first.

## 7.2 Authority / epistemic state

A record must distinguish what kind of claim it contains.

Suggested values:

```text
HUMAN_APPROVED
SYSTEM_OBSERVED
AGENT_INFERRED
EXTERNAL_RETRIEVED
DERIVED_SUMMARY
VALIDATED_LESSON
```

An agent inference must not silently become a human decision.

## 7.3 Knowledge links

Support typed relationships such as:

```text
derived_from
supersedes
related_to
applies_to
result_of
failed_because
fixed_by
validated_by
summarizes
```

This supports trajectory without inventing a complex graph database.

---

# 8. Failure learning is a first-class DKMS feature

The architecture workbook explicitly identifies failure/recovery knowledge as high value and potentially useful for future model training.

For each important failure, preserve:

```text
failure signature
failure class
system/service
Work/Jira/agent/session correlation
symptoms
observed evidence
attempted method
attempt number
result
side effects
time to recovery
root cause
final fix
preventive action
rollback used
confidence
validation status
related runbook/playbook
```

## 8.1 Learning lifecycle

Do not let a model promote one guess into permanent truth.

Use:

```text
OBSERVED
→ ANALYZED
→ LESSON_PROPOSED
→ VALIDATED
→ PLAYBOOK_PROMOTED
```

A validated lesson may update a recovery playbook.

The old evidence remains linked and immutable.

## 8.2 Retry learning

The existing direction is:

```text
runtime recovery: up to 5 attempts before Human Attention
failed work reassignment: up to 3 attempts before Human Attention
```

Before retrying failed work, prefer changing in this order:

```text
1. input: instructions / context / implementation strategy
2. brain: model
3. worker: agent
4. human clarification
```

DKMS should make previous attempts retrievable so retries do not blindly repeat the same unsuccessful action.

---

# 9. Ingestion policy

## 9.1 Human-approved plans

When an approved instruction plan is dispatched:

```text
dispatch Work
AND
asynchronously ingest approved plan into DKMS
```

The ingestion process must not significantly delay dispatch.

Record:

- content hash;
- source filename;
- Work/Jira relationship;
- approval provenance;
- timestamp;
- version/supersession relation.

## 9.2 Final handoffs

On Work completion or explicit handoff:

1. store or reference the source handoff;
2. create a concise durable summary;
3. correlate Jira / Work / agent / session / PR / commit / deployment / artifacts;
4. mark unresolved items;
5. link to the prior plan;
6. retain the summary indefinitely unless policy changes.

## 9.3 Jira

DKMS is **not** a Jira analytics warehouse.

For Jira-backed implementation, retain only enough durable knowledge to reconstruct the implementation trajectory:

- Jira key/title/reference;
- date the work entered execution or was materially scoped;
- short scope/acceptance summary;
- material scope-change summary when it changes architecture/implementation direction;
- date completed/reviewed;
- short outcome/what-was-implemented summary;
- links to relevant plan, handoff, PR/commit/deployment, and DKMS records.

Do **not** continuously copy Jira comments, status history, detailed Work metrics, assignee statistics, or other operational data into DKMS merely because it exists.

The goal is to understand **what changed, when it changed, and how the implementation trajectory evolved** so future agents can infer reasonable next work. Jira remains authoritative for the ticket itself.

## 9.4 ACMS

ACMS should publish knowledge-worthy events, not every heartbeat.

Candidate knowledge-worthy events:

```text
WORK_ACCEPTED              -> optional short implementation-timeline entry
SCOPE_CHANGED              -> only when material
HANDOFF_PUBLISHED          -> durable handoff + summary
WORK_REVIEWED              -> short implementation outcome/timeline entry
WORK_FAILED                -> failure-learning candidate
RECOVERY_EXHAUSTED         -> failure-learning candidate
HUMAN_ATTENTION            -> only when it creates durable lesson/context
LESSON_VALIDATED           -> durable lesson
```

**User decision:** ACMS must provide an explicit path for agents/operators to **Log Lesson Learned*

# AGENTS.md — DKMS

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in DKMS.

## Read before planning

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
4. `docs/SERVICE_BOUNDARY.md`
5. relevant ADRs in `docs/adr/`
6. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope and significant decisions. Agents may inspect, implement approved requirements, write tests, update documentation, create handoffs, and add discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`. Agents must not silently expand scope, accept their own significant ADR, weaken auth/audit, or perform destructive actions.

## Non-negotiable safety rules

1. **Secrets are not knowledge.** Never ingest, store, log, or export password values, API keys, bearer tokens, cookies, private keys, credential-bearing env files, or logs exposing secrets. Ingestion must reject/quarantine likely secret material (`training_eligibility=denied`, metadata only).
2. DKMS is **not** a Work-statistics or telemetry warehouse. ACMS owns Work statistics/status; Jira owns tickets; GitHub owns code; LLM Manager owns model facts; Server Manager owns runtime lifecycle; PDU Manager owns physical power. Record concise summaries + provenance, never bulk mirrors.
3. Records are immutable once created. Corrections happen through new versions + `supersedes` links; old evidence remains.
4. Every write must carry provenance: source kind/URI/UID, agent identity, authority class, scope.
5. Training eligibility defaults: validated internal failure/recovery lessons → `approved`/`auto_policy`/`not_reviewed`; raw transcripts → `denied`; unvalidated inferences → `review_required`; human review state is preserved and never overwritten by later auto-approval.
6. Retention: raw source content = 90 days default; durable summaries/decisions/lessons = indefinite. Purge is policy-driven + audited + dry-run first.
7. Deletion is retention-driven or explicit administrative action with audit. No agent-initiated destructive deletion.
8. Internal LAN only; no public ingress. Never weaken auth to simplify integration.

## Git workflow

Feature work on a dedicated branch (`type/dkms-short-description`), PR, tests green before merge. Commit format: `type(scope): short description (DKM-##)`. Agents do not self-merge unless a session-scoped authorization says otherwise.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work produces a Markdown handoff: goal, what changed, validation evidence, current state, blockers, next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit decisions may be `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions stay `Proposed` until human approval.

[Subdirectory context discovered: work/acms-project-framework/AGENTS.md]
# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"output": "\"\"\"SQLAlchemy 2.x engine/session factories for the Server Manager databases.\n\nTwo logical databases (§1): llmmanager (existing) + agent_runtime_manager (new).\nBoth may share the same PG host; credentials are separate; no cross-database FKs.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom sqlalchemy import create_engine, event\nfrom sqlalchemy.engine import Engine\nfrom sqlalchemy.orm import Session, sessionmaker\n\nfrom server_manager.common.config.settings import get_settings\n\n_engines: dict[str, Engine] = {}\n_sessions: dict[str, sessionmaker] = {}\n\n\ndef _make_url(dsn_host: str, port: int, db: str, user: str, password: str) -> str:\n    from urllib.parse import quote_plus\n\n    if dsn_host.startswith(\"/\"):\n        # unix socket directory: must travel in the query string, not the netloc\n        # (a leading '/' there is parsed as the database path, host=None).\n        return (f\"<DSN-redacted>/{db}\"\n                f\"?host={quote_plus(dsn_host)}&port={int(port)}\")\n    return f\"<DSN-redacted>{dsn_host}:{port}/{db}\"\n\n\ndef get_engine(db: str = \"arm\") -> Engine:\n    \"\"\"db='arm' → agent_runtime_manager DB; db='llm' → llmmanager DB.\"\"\"\n    if db in _engines:\n        return _engines[db]\n    s = get_settings()\n    if db == \"arm\":\n        url = _make_url(s.arm_pg_host, s.arm_pg_port, s.arm_pg_db, s.arm_pg_user, s.arm_pg_password)\n    elif db == \"llm\":\n        url = _make_url(s.llm_pg_host, s.llm_pg_port, s.llm_pg_db, s.llm_pg_user, s.llm_pg_password)\n    elif db == \"litellm\":\n        # LiteLLM proxy's own DB (SpendLogs). Same PG host, same app role (pg_app_creds),\n        # separate logical database per the existing app's own dual-DSN pattern (main.py:609).", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "\"\"\"Scoped service authentication (plan §11).\n\nMVP: scoped bearer credentials from a root-owned 0600 file, private network,\naudited, rotatable outside Git. Token file format:\n\n    # service   = <token>\n    svc-acms    = <token>\n    svc-server-manager = <token>\n\nEach caller identity carries an action scope. Documented TDR: temporary\nservice token → future mTLS (plan §11/§19).\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport hmac\nimport os\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom functools import lru_cache\n\nfrom fastapi import HTTPException, Request\n\n# Scope names (least-privilege defaults per §10B/§13)\nSCOPES_READ = {\"runtime:read\", \"job:read\", \"route:read\", \"usage:read\", \"health:read\"}\nSCOPES_WRITE = {\"runtime:write\", \"runtime:destroy\", \"job:submit\"}\nALL_SCOPES = SCOPES_READ | SCOPES_WRITE\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ServiceIdentity:\n    service: str\n    scopes: frozenset[str]\n\n    def can(self, scope: str) -> bool:\n        return scope in self.scopes\n\n\ndef _load_identities() -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    \"\"\"Returns {bearer_token: (service, scopes)} from the tokens file.\n\n    File format (0600, outside Git):\n        # service=token:scope1,scope2\n        svc-acms=token1:runtime:read,runtime:write,job:read,job:submit\n        svc-server-manager=token2:ALL\n    \"\"\"\n    path = os.environ.get(\"SERVER_MANAGER_SERVICE_TOKENS_FILE\", \"/etc/llm-manager/secrets/service_tokens\")\n    out: dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]] = {}\n    try:\n        with open(path) as f:\n            for line in f:\n                line = line.strip()\n                if not line or line.startswith(\"#\") or \"=\" not in line:\n                    continue\n                service, rest = line.split(\"=\", 1)\n                token, _, scope_s = rest.partition(\":\")\n                token = token.strip()\n                if not token:\n                    continue\n                if scope_s.strip() in (\"\", \"ALL\"):\n                    scopes = frozenset(ALL_SCOPES)", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "scopes = frozenset(ALL_SCOPES)\n                else:\n                    scopes = frozenset(x.strip() for x in scope_s.split(\",\") if x.strip())\n                out[token] = (service.strip(), scopes)\n    except OSError:\n        pass\n    return out\n\n\n@lru_cache(maxsize=1)\ndef _identities_cached(mtime: float) -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    return _load_identities()\n\n\ndef _identities() -> dict[str, tuple[str, frozenset[str]]]:\n    path = os.environ.get(\"SERVER_MANAGER_SERVICE_TOKENS_FILE\", \"/etc/llm-manager/secrets/service_tokens\")\n    try:\n        mtime = os.stat(path).st_mtime\n    except OSError:\n        mtime = 0\n    return _identities_cached(mtime)\n\n\ndef constant_time_lookup(token: str) -> ServiceIdentity | None:\n    identities = _identities()\n    for tok, (service, scopes) in identities.items():\n        if hmac.compare_digest(tok, token):\n            return ServiceIdentity(service=service, scopes=scopes)\n    return None\n\n\ndef require_identity(request: Request, scope: str) -> ServiceIdentity:\n    \"\"\"FastAPI dependency: verify bearer token + scope; 401 first, then 403.\"\"\"\n    auth = request.headers.get(\"Authorization\", \"\")\n    if not auth.startswith(\"Bearer \"):\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=\"missing bearer credential\")\n    token = auth[len(\"Bearer \"):].strip()\n    ident = constant_time_lookup(token)\n    if ident is None:\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail=\"invalid credential\")\n    if not ident.can(scope):\n        raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail=f\"identity '{ident.service}' lacks scope '{scope}'\")\n    return ident", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

quoted assignments\n    re.compile(r\"(?i)password\\s*[=:]\\s*['\\\"][^'\\\"]{8,}['\\\"]\"),\n    re.compile(r\"(?i)token\\s*[=:]\\s*['\\\"][^'\\\"]{20,}['\\\"]\"),\n    re.compile(r\"(?i)api[_-]?key\\s*[=:]\\s*['\\\"][^'\\\"]{16,}['\\\"]\"),\n    # unquoted assignments (config/env style): password=hunter2..., api_key=...\n    re.compile(r\"(?i)\\bpassword\\s*[=:]\\s*\\S{8,}\"),\n    re.compile(r\"(?i)\\bapi[_-]?key\\s*[=:]\\s*\\S{16,}\"),\n    re.compile(r\"(?i)\\bsecret\\s*[=:]\\s*\\S{16,}\"),\n    re.compile(r\"(?i)bearer\\s+[a-zA-Z0-9_\\-\\.]{20,}\"),\n]\n\n\ndef contains_secret(content: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Return True if content appears to contain secrets.\"\"\"\n    for pattern in SECRET_PATTERNS:\n        if pattern.search(content):\n            return True\n    return False\n\n\ndef describe_secret_match(content: str) -> str | None:\n    \"\"\"Return description of matched secret type, or None if no match.\"\"\"\n    descriptions = [\n        (r\"sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{20,}\", \"OpenAI API key\"),\n        (r\"ghp_[a-zA-Z0-9]{36,}\", \"GitHub personal access token\"),\n        (r\"github_pat_[a-zA-Z0-9_]{22,}\", \"GitHub fine-grained PAT\"),\n        (r\"gho_|ghu_|ghr_|ghs_\", \"GitHub OAuth/App token\"),\n        (r\"AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}\", \"AWS access key\"),\n        (r\"-----BEGIN.*PRIVATE\\s+KEY-----\", \"Private key block\"),\n        (r\"password\\s*[=:]\", \"Password assignment\"),\n        (r\"token\\s*[=:]\", \"Token assignment\"),\n        (r\"api_key\\s*[=:]\", \"API key assignment\"),\n        (r\"bearer\\s+\", \"Bearer token\"),\n    ]\n    for pattern, desc in descriptions:\n        if re.search(pattern, content, re.IGNORECASE):\n            return desc\n    return None", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Plain HTTP on the LAN NodePort; TLS when\n    base_url starts with https (future DNS/TLS, same pattern as AcmsClient).\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, base_url: str, token: str, *, timeout_seconds: int = 15):\n        if not base_url:\n            raise ValueError(\"DKMS base URL not configured\")\n        if not token:\n            raise ValueError(\"DKMS service token not configured\")\n        self.base_url = base_url.rstrip(\"/\")\n        self._token = token\n        self.timeout = timeout_seconds\n        if self.base_url.startswith(\"https://\"):\n            self._ctx: ssl.SSLContext | None = ssl.create_default_context()\n        else:\n            self._ctx = None\n\n    def _request(self, method: str, path: str, payload: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n                 query: dict[str, str] | None = None, *, auth: bool = True,\n                 accept: str = \"application/json\") -> tuple[int, Any]:\n        url = f\"{self.base_url}{path}\"\n        if query:\n            from urllib.parse import urlencode\n            filtered = {k: v for k, v in query.items() if v is not None and v != \"\"}\n            if filtered:\n                url += \"?\" + urlencode(filtered)\n        data = None\n        headers: dict[str, str] = {\"Accept\": accept}\n        if auth:\n            headers[\"Authorization\"] = f\"Bearer {self._token}\"\n        if payload is not None:", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Install/upgrade the MIAM MCP gateway on VM114 (Window 1).\n# Usage: bash deploy/mcp-gateway/install-gateway.sh [repo-checkout-on-vm114]\n# Assumes: repo at /opt/mcp-gateway/repo (rsynced from the workstation), env at\n# /etc/miam-mcp-gateway/env (0600). Creates /var/lib/miam-mcp-gateway.\nset -euo pipefail\n\nREPO_DIR=\"${1:-/opt/mcp-gateway/repo}\"\nGATEWAY_DIR=/var/lib/miam-mcp-gateway\nENV_FILE=/etc/miam-mcp-gateway/env\n\nfail() { echo \"FAIL: $*\" >&2; exit 1; }\n\n[ -d \"$REPO_DIR/mcp_gateway\" ] || fail \"mcp_gateway package not found in $REPO_DIR\"\nmkdir -p \"$GATEWAY_DIR\" /etc/miam-mcp-gateway\nchmod 700 \"$GATEWAY_DIR\" /etc/miam-mcp-gateway\n\n# python deps into a dedicated venv (PEP 668-safe)\nif [ ! -x \"$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp/bin/python\" ]; then\n  python3 -m venv \"$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp\"\n  \"$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp/bin/pip\" install --quiet 'mcp>=1.26,<2'\nfi\n\n# unit file with the venv python\nsed -e \"s|ExecStart=/usr/bin/python3|ExecStart=$REPO_DIR/.venv-mcp/bin/python|\" \\\n  -e \"s|WorkingDirectory=/opt/mcp-gateway/repo|WorkingDirectory=$REPO_DIR|\" \\\n  -e \"s|Environment=PYTHONPATH=/opt/mcp-gateway/repo|Environment=PYTHONPATH=$REPO_DIR|\" \\\n  \"$REPO_DIR/deploy/mcp-gateway/miam-mcp-gateway.service\" > /etc/systemd/system/miam-mcp-gateway.service\n\n[ -f \"$ENV_FILE\" ] || { echo \"NOTE: $ENV_FILE missing — create it from env.example before first start\"; }\nchmod 600 \"$ENV_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true\n\nsystemctl daemon-reload\nsystemctl enable --now miam-mcp-gateway\nsleep 2\nsystemctl --no-pager is-active miam-mcp-gateway\ncurl -fsS http://127.0.0.1:8202/health && echo && echo \"GATEWAY-INSTALL-OK\"", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "\"\"\"Bearer token authentication dependency.\"\"\"\n\nfrom fastapi import Depends, HTTPException, Request, status\nfrom fastapi.security import HTTPAuthorizationCredentials, HTTPBearer\n\nfrom dkms.config import get_settings\n\nbearer_scheme = HTTPBearer(auto_error=False)\n\n\nasync def require_auth(\n    request: Request,\n    credentials: HTTPAuthorizationCredentials | None = Depends(bearer_scheme),\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Require valid bearer token. Fail-closed when token not configured.\"\"\"\n    settings = get_settings()\n\n    if settings.api_token is None:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_503_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE,\n            detail=\"Service not configured: API token not set\",\n        )\n\n    if credentials is None:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,\n            detail=\"Missing authorization header\",\n            headers={\"WWW-Authenticate\": \"Bearer\"},\n        )\n\n    if credentials.credentials != settings.api_token:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_401_UNAUTHORIZED,\n            detail=\"Invalid token\",\n            headers={\"WWW-Authenticate\": \"Bearer\"},\n        )\n\n    return credentials.credentials", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Reusable PVE qga helper (workstation-side). Usage:\n  pve_qga.py exec <node> <vmid> '<bash-cmd>'   -> prints stdout, exits with guest exit code\n  pve_qga.py write <node> <vmid> <local-path> <guest-path>  -> base64-stage + decode + sha check\n\"\"\"\nimport json, ssl, sys, time, base64, hashlib, urllib.request, urllib.parse\n\nPVE = 'https://10.0.20.100:8006'\nCTX = ssl.create_default_context(); CTX.check_hostname = False; CTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\n\ndef _login():\n    lines = open('/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/creds/pve_root.txt').read().split()\n    user = lines[0] if '@' in lines[0] else lines[0] + '@pam'\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({'username': user, 'password': lines[1]}).encode()\n    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + '/api2/json/access/ticket', data=data, method='POST')\n    r = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=15))['data']\n    return r['ticket'], r['CSRFPreventionToken']\n\nTICKET, CSRF = _login()\n\ndef api(method, path, payload=None):\n    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={TICKET}'}\n    if method in ('POST', 'PUT', 'DELETE'):\n        headers['CSRFPreventionToken'] = CSRF\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode(payload, doseq=True).encode() if payload is not None else None\n    last = None\n    for attempt in range(4):\n        try:\n            req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + path, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)\n            return json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=90))\n        except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:\n            if e.code in (596, 502, 503) and attempt < 3:\n                time.sleep(2 + attempt * 2)\n                last = e\n                continue\n            raise\n        except urllib.error.URLError as e:\n            if attempt < 3:\n                time.sleep(2 + attempt * 2)\n                last = e\n                continue\n            raise\n    raise last\n\ndef exec_wait(node, vmid, cmd, timeout=90):\n    r = api('POST', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec',\n            {'command': ['/bin/bash', '-c', cmd]})\n    pid = r['data']['pid']\n    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n    while time.time() < deadline:", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "session: AsyncSession,\n    source_kind: str,\n    source_uid: str,\n    content: str,\n    title: str,\n    record_type: str = \"source-artifact\",\n    **extra_fields,\n) -> tuple[KnowledgeRecord, bool]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Ingest source content idempotently.\n\n    Returns (record, created) where created=False means existing record was found.\n    \"\"\"\n    content_sha = compute_content_hash(content)\n\n    stmt = select(KnowledgeRecord).where(\n        KnowledgeRecord.source_uid == source_uid,\n        KnowledgeRecord.content_sha256 == content_sha,\n    )\n    result = await session.execute(stmt)\n    existing = result.scalar_one_or_none()\n\n    if existing:\n        return existing, False\n\n    now = datetime.utcnow()\n    retention_class = extra_fields.pop(\"retention_class\", None) or classify_retention(record_type)\n    expires_at = compute_expires_at(retention_class, now)\n\n    record = KnowledgeRecord(\n        knowledge_uid=generate_uid(),\n        record_type=record_type,\n        title=title,\n        body_markdown=content,\n        content_sha256=content_sha,\n        source_kind=source_kind,\n        source_uid=source_uid,\n        retention_class=retention_class,\n        expires_at=expires_at,\n        **extra_fields,\n    )\n    session.add(record)\n    await session.commit()\n    await session.refresh(record)\n    return record, True", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Must be one of: {sorted(VALID_RECORD_TYPES)}\",\n        )\n\n    if contains_secret(data.body_markdown) or contains_secret(data.title):\n        secret_type = describe_secret_match(data.body_markdown) or describe_secret_match(data.title)\n        await _audit(\n            session,\n            actor=\"api\",\n            action=\"secret_rejected\",\n            target_uid=None,\n            detail={\"record_type\": data.record_type, \"secret_type\": secret_type},\n        )\n        await session.commit()\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_422_UNPROCESSABLE_ENTITY,\n            detail=\"QUARANTINED: Content contains potential secrets\",\n        )\n\n    content_sha = compute_content_hash(data.body_markdown)\n    knowledge_uid = generate_uid()\n\n    retention_class = data.retention_class\n    if retention_class is None:\n        retention_class = classify_retention(data.record_type)\n\n    training_eligibility = data.training_eligibility\n    if training_eligibility is None:\n        if data.record_type == \"lesson_learned\":\n            training_eligibility = \"review_required\"\n        elif data.record_type in RAW_SOURCE_TYPES:\n            training_eligibility = \"denied\"\n        else:\n            training_eligibility = \"review_required\"\n\n    now = datetime.utcnow()\n    expires_at = compute_expires_at(retention_class, now)\n\n    record = KnowledgeRecord(\n        knowledge_uid=knowledge_uid,\n        record_type=data.record_type,\n        title=data.title,\n        body_markdown=data.body_markdown,\n        content_sha256=content_sha,\n        source_kind=data.source_kind,\n        source_uri=data.source_uri,\n        source_uid=data.source_uid,\n        source_version=data.source_version,\n        source_created_at=data.source_created_at,\n        project_uid=data.project_uid,\n        product_uid=data.product_uid,\n        work_uid=data.work_uid,\n        jira_key=data.jira_key,\n        agent_uid=data.agent_uid,\n        session_uid=data.session_uid,\n        repository=data.repository,\n        commit_sha=data.commit_sha,\n        authority_class=data.authority_class,\n        confidence=data.confidence,\n        sensitivity_class=data.sensitivity_class,\n        training_eligibility=training_eligibility,\n        training_approval_mode=data.training_approval_mode,\n        training_eligibility_reason=data.training_eligibility_reason,\n        retention_class=retention_class,\n        expires_at=expires_at,\n        supersedes_uid=data.supersedes_uid,\n        tags=data.tags,\n    )\n    session.add(record)", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

---

### [2026-10-03T11:59:00.793574+00:00] assistant

**Root cause confirmed:** the SDK's `matches()` naively converts `{...}` to regex — a bare `?` in the URI template (`dkms://search?q={query}`) becomes a **regex quantifier** (`h?` = optional 'h'), so the query-string templates can never match. This is a gateway-side bug affecting any domain using `?key={var}` templates. The fix: escape the `?` (and other regex specials) in the gateway's registration path — i.e., regex-escape the literal parts of the template before the `{...}` substitution. Let me check how `add_template` is invoked and patch the gateway (fix belongs in gateway code since it affects all domains):

---
